Um… I guess it’s been harder to keep up lately. I’m a hairstylist, and at work my hands just feel… slow, like they’re moving through glue or something. Even simple things like sectioning hair or mixing color take me way longer than they should.

I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but people have started noticing I’m not as quick as I used to be, so… I thought maybe I should come in. Is that the kind of thing I should mention?

[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n2\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nG24.9, F48.8, R48.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess it’s been harder to keep up lately. I’m a hairstylist, and at work my hands just feel… slow, like they’re moving through glue or something. Even simple things like sectioning hair or mixing color take me way longer than they should.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but people have started noticing I’m not as quick as I used to be, so… I thought maybe I should come in. Is that the kind of thing I should mention?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel slow or like your hands are moving through glue only at work, or does it happen at other times too?\n\nPatient: Mostly I notice it at work, because that’s where it really shows. Like sectioning hair or mixing color used to be automatic, and now it feels like my hands are kind of moving through glue and it takes me forever.\n\nI mean, maybe “forever” is a little dramatic, but… people have started noticing I’m slower than I used to be, so I guess it’s not just in my head. At home… I mean, I’m mostly getting by, I think. It’s not like everything has fallen apart or anything.

But I guess the bigger daily stuff I used to handle… I’ve been dropping the ball more. At work my manager doesn’t really trust me with things the way she used to, because I’m making mistakes with basic salon routines or taking too long, and I’ve canceled things I normally would’ve been reliable for.

And outside of work, I’ve kind of stopped showing up for things… like I used to do volunteer haircuts at the senior center, and I haven’t been going. I’ve also pulled back from friends some. I don’t know if that counts as “daily activities at home,” exactly, but it’s been affecting what I keep up with.

Is that the kind of thing I should mention?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel slow or like your hands are moving through glue only at work, or does it happen at other times too?\n\nPatient: Mostly I notice it at work, because that’s where it really shows. Like sectioning hair or mixing color used to be automatic, and now it feels like my hands are kind of moving through glue and it takes me forever.\n\nI mean, maybe “forever” is a little dramatic, but… people have started noticing I’m slower than I used to be, so I guess it’s not just in my head. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel slow or like you're moving through glue when you're doing things that don't involve using your hands, like walking or talking?\n\nPatient: Um… I mostly notice it with my hands, honestly. Like at work, sectioning hair or mixing color feels like my hands are moving through glue, and it takes me way longer than it used to.\n\nI don’t know if I’d say my walking or talking is slow… not that I’ve noticed as much. It’s more that people at work have noticed I’m just slower with what I’m doing. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tired or exhausted?\n\nPatient: I mean… I get tired, but I don’t know if it’s more than normal. Work can be long, so I kind of just figured that’s why. I wouldn’t want to overstate it. I guess I haven’t really paid close attention to whether I’m exhausted a lot. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble concentrating or paying attention?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but at work I’ll be looking at a color formula or the appointment screen and my brain just kind of… blanks.\n\nThen I start second-guessing simple stuff I used to do automatically, like, “Wait, is that the right mix?” or “What was I supposed to do next?” It’s embarrassing, honestly. Is that the kind of concentration thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are you having any trouble with daily activities at home?\n\nPatient: At home… I mean, I’m mostly getting by, I think. It’s not like everything has fallen apart or anything.\n\nBut I guess the bigger daily stuff I used to handle… I’ve been dropping the ball more. At work my manager doesn’t really trust me with things the way she used to, because I’m making mistakes with basic salon routines or taking too long, and I’ve canceled things I normally would’ve been reliable for.\n\nAnd outside of work, I’ve kind of stopped showing up for things… like I used to do volunteer haircuts at the senior center, and I haven’t been going. I’ve also pulled back from friends some. I don’t know if that counts as “daily activities at home,” exactly, but it’s been affecting what I keep up with.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or overwhelmed lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure… I mean, I get stressed, yeah, but I don’t know if I’d call it anxious exactly. Maybe overwhelmed sometimes when there’s a lot going on, but I kind of assume that’s normal?\n\nI don’t want to make it sound like I’m falling apart or anything. What do you mean by overwhelmed—like panic, or just feeling like things are too much?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. I don’t want to sound dramatic, but most days I wake up and there’s this kind of heavy, flat sadness in my chest.\n\nIt’s like… I look at the life I was trying to build, and it feels hard to see the point in trying. I know that probably sounds bad. Is that what you mean by sad or down?\n\nDoctor: Is this heavy, flat sadness you feel in your chest usually present all day, or are there times when it lifts a bit?\n\nPatient: It’s… usually kind of there most of the day, I think. I mean, it can ease up a little if I’m distracted or trying to act normal, but when I stop, it’s still there.\n\nMost mornings I wake up with that heavy, flat feeling in my chest, and it just makes everything I was planning for my life feel kind of pointless. I don’t know if that’s the right way to explain it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.   },

Is that the kind of thing I should mention?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel slow or like your hands are moving through glue only at work, or does it happen at other times too?\n\nPatient: Mostly I notice it at work, because that’s where it really shows. Like sectioning hair or mixing color used to be automatic, and now it feels like my hands are kind of moving through glue and it takes me forever.\n\nI mean, maybe “forever” is a little dramatic, but… people have started noticing I’m slower than I used to be, so I guess it’s not just in my head. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel slow or like you're moving through glue when you're doing things that don't involve using your hands, like walking or talking?\n\nPatient: Um… I mostly notice it with my hands, honestly. Like at work, sectioning hair or mixing color feels like my hands are moving through glue, and it takes me way longer than it used to.\n\nI don’t know if I’d say my walking or talking is slow… not that I’ve noticed as much. It’s more that people at work have noticed I’m just slower with what I’m doing. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tired or exhausted?\n\nPatient: I mean… I get tired, but I don’t know if it’s more than normal. Work can be long, so I kind of just figured that’s why. I wouldn’t want to overstate it. I guess I haven’t really paid close attention to whether I’m exhausted a lot. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble concentrating or paying attention?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but at work I’ll be looking at a color formula or the appointment screen and my brain just kind of… blanks.\n\nThen I start second-guessing simple stuff I used to do automatically, like, “Wait, is that the right mix?” or “What was I supposed to do next?” It’s embarrassing, honestly. Is that the kind of concentration thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are you having any trouble with daily activities at home?\n\nPatient: At home… I mean, I’m mostly getting by, I think. It’s not like everything has fallen apart or anything.\n\nBut I guess the bigger daily stuff I used to handle… I’ve been dropping the ball more. At work my manager doesn’t really trust me with things the way she used to, because I’m making mistakes with basic salon routines or taking too long, and I’ve canceled things I normally would’ve been reliable for.\n\nAnd outside of work, I’ve kind of stopped showing up for things… like I used to do volunteer haircuts at the senior center, and I haven’t been going. I’ve also pulled back from friends some. I don’t know if that counts as “daily activities at home,” exactly, but it’s been affecting what I keep up with.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or overwhelmed lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure… I mean, I get stressed, yeah, but I don’t know if I’d call it anxious exactly. Maybe overwhelmed sometimes when there’s a lot going on, but I kind of assume that’s normal?\n\nI don’t want to make it sound like I’m falling apart or anything. What do you mean by overwhelmed—like panic, or just feeling like things are too much?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. I don’t want to sound dramatic, but most days I wake up and there’s this kind of heavy, flat sadness in my chest.\n\nIt’s like… I look at the life I was trying to build, and it feels hard to see the point in trying. I know that probably sounds bad. Is that what you mean by sad or down?\n\nDoctor: Is this heavy, flat sadness you feel in your chest usually present all day, or are there times when it lifts a bit?\n\nPatient: It’s… usually kind of there most of the day, I think. I mean, it can ease up a little if I’m distracted or trying to act normal, but when I stop, it’s still there.\n\nMost mornings I wake up with that heavy, flat feeling in my chest, and it just makes everything I was planning for my life feel kind of pointless. I don’t know if that’s the right way to explain it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're still enjoying the things you used to like doing?\n\nPatient: I still like the idea of them, I guess… but I don’t really do them the same way anymore.\n\nAt work I used to chat with clients more, and I’d help the newer stylists without thinking about it. And I used to go hiking with friends sometimes. Lately I mostly just keep my head down and try to get through the day. I avoid people more than I probably should.\n\nIt’s not that I hate everything… I just don’t feel that spark in me like I used to. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about death or wanting to die?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think I’d say I want to die exactly. I don’t have, like, a plan to hurt myself or anything.\n\nBut… sometimes I do get thoughts like it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up. Like maybe nobody would have to deal with me anymore. I know that sounds awful… I’m sorry. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

At work my manager doesn’t really trust me with things the way she used to, because I’m making mistakes with basic salon routines or taking too long, and I’ve canceled things I normally would’ve been reliable for.\n\nAnd outside of work, I’ve kind of stopped showing up for things… like I used to do volunteer haircuts at the senior center, and I haven’t been going. I’ve also pulled back from friends some. I don’t know if that counts as “daily activities at home,” exactly, but it’s been affecting what I keep up with.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or overwhelmed lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure… I mean, I get stressed, yeah, but I don’t know if I’d call it anxious exactly. Maybe overwhelmed sometimes when there’s a lot going on, but I kind of assume that’s normal?\n\nI don’t want to make it sound like I’m falling apart or anything. I don’t want to sound dramatic, but most days I wake up and there’s this kind of heavy, flat sadness in my chest.\n\nIt’s like… I look at the life I was trying to build, and it feels hard to see the point in trying. I know that probably sounds bad. I don’t know if that’s the right way to explain it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're still enjoying the things you used to like doing?\n\nPatient: I still like the idea of them, I guess… but I don’t really do them the same way anymore.\n\nAt work I used to chat with clients more, and I’d help the newer stylists without thinking about it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about death or wanting to die?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think I’d say I want to die exactly. I know that sounds awful… I’m sorry. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, flat sadness in your chest?\n\nPatient: It’s been… kind of a long time. It’s… more than just in the background, I think. I don’t want to sound like I’m making excuses, but it’s been affecting things.

I don’t want to sound dramatic, but most days I wake up and there’s this kind of heavy, flat sadness in my chest.\n\nIt’s like… I look at the life I was trying to build, and it feels hard to see the point in trying. I know that probably sounds bad. Is that what you mean by sad or down?\n\nDoctor: Is this heavy, flat sadness you feel in your chest usually present all day, or are there times when it lifts a bit?\n\nPatient: It’s… usually kind of there most of the day, I think. I mean, it can ease up a little if I’m distracted or trying to act normal, but when I stop, it’s still there.\n\nMost mornings I wake up with that heavy, flat feeling in my chest, and it just makes everything I was planning for my life feel kind of pointless. I don’t know if that’s the right way to explain it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're still enjoying the things you used to like doing?\n\nPatient: I still like the idea of them, I guess… but I don’t really do them the same way anymore.\n\nAt work I used to chat with clients more, and I’d help the newer stylists without thinking about it. I avoid people more than I probably should.\n\nIt’s not that I hate everything… I just don’t feel that spark in me like I used to. I know that sounds awful… I’m sorry. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, flat sadness in your chest?\n\nPatient: It’s been… kind of a long time. I think about a year and two months, maybe. I kept thinking it would pass or that I was just tired, so I didn’t really want to make a big deal out of it. But yeah… around that long.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last two weeks where you felt okay, or has the sadness been constant?\n\nPatient: I’ve had little moments where I can act okay, I guess… like if I’m busy or I don’t want people to worry. But most days, honestly, I wake up with this heavy, flat sadness in my chest.\n\nIt’s like… I know I had plans for my life, and then it feels like there’s no point trying to build any of it. I’m sorry, that sounds dramatic. But it’s been there most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last two months where you felt significantly better, or has the sadness been constant?\n\nPatient: I mean… there have been small breaks, I guess, where I can distract myself and seem more normal. Is that the kind of thing I should mention?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel slow or like your hands are moving through glue only at work, or does it happen at other times too?\n\nPatient: Mostly I notice it at work, because that’s where it really shows. Like sectioning hair or mixing color used to be automatic, and now it feels like my hands are kind of moving through glue and it takes me forever.\n\nI mean, maybe “forever” is a little dramatic, but… people have started noticing I’m slower than I used to be, so I guess it’s not just in my head. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel slow or like you're moving through glue when you're doing things that don't involve using your hands, like walking or talking?\n\nPatient: Um… I mostly notice it with my hands, honestly. Like at work, sectioning hair or mixing color feels like my hands are moving through glue, and it takes me way longer than it used to.\n\nI don’t know if I’d say my walking or talking is slow… not that I’ve noticed as much. It’s more that people at work have noticed I’m just slower with what I’m doing. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tired or exhausted?\n\nPatient: I mean… I get tired, but I don’t know if it’s more than normal. Work can be long, so I kind of just figured that’s why. I wouldn’t want to overstate it. I guess I haven’t really paid close attention to whether I’m exhausted a lot. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble concentrating or paying attention?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but at work I’ll be looking at a color formula or the appointment screen and my brain just kind of… blanks.\n\nThen I start second-guessing simple stuff I used to do automatically, like, “Wait, is that the right mix?” or “What was I supposed to do next?” It’s embarrassing, honestly. Is that the kind of concentration thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are you having any trouble with daily activities at home?\n\nPatient: At home… I mean, I’m mostly getting by, I think. It’s not like everything has fallen apart or anything.\n\nBut I guess the bigger daily stuff I used to handle… I’ve been dropping the ball more. At work my manager doesn’t really trust me with things the way she used to, because I’m making mistakes with basic salon routines or taking too long, and I’ve canceled things I normally would’ve been reliable for.\n\nAnd outside of work, I’ve kind of stopped showing up for things… like I used to do volunteer haircuts at the senior center, and I haven’t been going. I’ve also pulled back from friends some. I don’t know if that counts as “daily activities at home,” exactly, but it’s been affecting what I keep up with.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or overwhelmed lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure… I mean, I get stressed, yeah, but I don’t know if I’d call it anxious exactly. Maybe overwhelmed sometimes when there’s a lot going on, but I kind of assume that’s normal?\n\nI don’t want to make it sound like I’m falling apart or anything. What do you mean by overwhelmed—like panic, or just feeling like things are too much?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. I don’t want to sound dramatic, but most days I wake up and there’s this kind of heavy, flat sadness in my chest.\n\nIt’s like… I look at the life I was trying to build, and it feels hard to see the point in trying. I know that probably sounds bad. Is that what you mean by sad or down?\n\nDoctor: Is this heavy, flat sadness you feel in your chest usually present all day, or are there times when it lifts a bit?\n\nPatient: It’s… usually kind of there most of the day, I think. I mean, it can ease up a little if I’m distracted or trying to act normal, but when I stop, it’s still there.\n\nMost mornings I wake up with that heavy, flat feeling in my chest, and it just makes everything I was planning for my life feel kind of pointless. I don’t know if that’s the right way to explain it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're still enjoying the things you used to like doing?\n\nPatient: I still like the idea of them, I guess… but I don’t really do them the same way anymore.\n\nAt work I used to chat with clients more, and I’d help the newer stylists without thinking about it. And I used to go hiking with friends sometimes. Lately I mostly just keep my head down and try to get through the day. I avoid people more than I probably should.\n\nIt’s not that I hate everything… I just don’t feel that spark in me like I used to. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about death or wanting to die?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think I’d say I want to die exactly. I don’t have, like, a plan to hurt myself or anything.\n\nBut… sometimes I do get thoughts like it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up. Like maybe nobody would have to deal with me anymore. I know that sounds awful… I’m sorry. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, flat sadness in your chest?\n\nPatient: It’s been… kind of a long time. I think about a year and two months, maybe. I kept thinking it would pass or that I was just tired, so I didn’t really want to make a big deal out of it. But yeah… around that long.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last two weeks where you felt okay, or has the sadness been constant?\n\nPatient: I’ve had little moments where I can act okay, I guess… like if I’m busy or I don’t want people to worry. But most days, honestly, I wake up with this heavy, flat sadness in my chest.\n\nIt’s like… I know I had plans for my life, and then it feels like there’s no point trying to build any of it. I’m sorry, that sounds dramatic. But it’s been there most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last two months where you felt significantly better, or has the sadness been constant?\n\nPatient: I mean… there have been small breaks, I guess, where I can distract myself and seem more normal. But I don’t think I’ve really felt significantly better.\n\nMost days I still wake up with that heavy, flat sadness in my chest… like there’s no point in trying to build the life I was planning. I’m sorry, I don’t mean to make it sound dramatic, but it’s been pretty constant.\n\nDoctor: Would you say this heavy, flat sadness is interfering with your daily life most of the time, or is it more of a mild feeling in the background?\n\nPatient: It’s… more than just in the background, I think. I don’t want to sound like I’m making excuses, but it’s been affecting things.\n\nAt work, I’m slower than I used to be, and I make these basic mistakes with salon routines that I really should know by now. My manager and some clients don’t really trust me the same way, and that feels awful. I’ve canceled things I used to handle without a problem.\n\nAnd outside of work I’ve kind of… pulled back from friends. I also stopped showing up for the volunteer haircuts at the senior center, which I used to be reliable with. So, yeah… I guess it is interfering most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: A little, maybe… I don’t want to say nothing, because that sounds bad. But it’s not like it used to be.\n\nI used to really like chatting with clients and helping the newer stylists at the salon, and I’d go hiking with my friends sometimes. Now I mostly just keep my head down and get through what I have to do. I kind of avoid people more than I mean to.\n\nIt’s not that I hate everything, I just… don’t really have that spark in me lately, if that makes sense.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Work can be long, so I kind of just figured that’s why. I wouldn’t want to overstate it. I guess I haven’t really paid close attention to whether I’m exhausted a lot. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble concentrating or paying attention?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but at work I’ll be looking at a color formula or the appointment screen and my brain just kind of… blanks.\n\nThen I start second-guessing simple stuff I used to do automatically, like, “Wait, is that the right mix?” or “What was I supposed to do next?” It’s embarrassing, honestly. Is that the kind of concentration thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are you having any trouble with daily activities at home?\n\nPatient: At home… I mean, I’m mostly getting by, I think. It’s not like everything has fallen apart or anything.\n\nBut I guess the bigger daily stuff I used to handle… I’ve been dropping the ball more. At work my manager doesn’t really trust me with things the way she used to, because I’m making mistakes with basic salon routines or taking too long, and I’ve canceled things I normally would’ve been reliable for.\n\nAnd outside of work, I’ve kind of stopped showing up for things… like I used to do volunteer haircuts at the senior center, and I haven’t been going. I’ve also pulled back from friends some. I don’t know if that counts as “daily activities at home,” exactly, but it’s been affecting what I keep up with.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or overwhelmed lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure… I mean, I get stressed, yeah, but I don’t know if I’d call it anxious exactly. Maybe overwhelmed sometimes when there’s a lot going on, but I kind of assume that’s normal?\n\nI don’t want to make it sound like I’m falling apart or anything. What do you mean by overwhelmed—like panic, or just feeling like things are too much?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. I don’t want to sound dramatic, but most days I wake up and there’s this kind of heavy, flat sadness in my chest.\n\nIt’s like… I look at the life I was trying to build, and it feels hard to see the point in trying. I know that probably sounds bad. Is that what you mean by sad or down?\n\nDoctor: Is this heavy, flat sadness you feel in your chest usually present all day, or are there times when it lifts a bit?\n\nPatient: It’s… usually kind of there most of the day, I think. I mean, it can ease up a little if I’m distracted or trying to act normal, but when I stop, it’s still there.\n\nMost mornings I wake up with that heavy, flat feeling in my chest, and it just makes everything I was planning for my life feel kind of pointless. I don’t know if that’s the right way to explain it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're still enjoying the things you used to like doing?\n\nPatient: I still like the idea of them, I guess… but I don’t really do them the same way anymore.\n\nAt work I used to chat with clients more, and I’d help the newer stylists without thinking about it. And I used to go hiking with friends sometimes. Lately I mostly just keep my head down and try to get through the day. I avoid people more than I probably should.\n\nIt’s not that I hate everything… I just don’t feel that spark in me like I used to. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about death or wanting to die?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think I’d say I want to die exactly. I don’t have, like, a plan to hurt myself or anything.\n\nBut… sometimes I do get thoughts like it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up. Like maybe nobody would have to deal with me anymore. I know that sounds awful… I’m sorry. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, flat sadness in your chest?\n\nPatient: It’s been… kind of a long time. I think about a year and two months, maybe. I kept thinking it would pass or that I was just tired, so I didn’t really want to make a big deal out of it. But yeah… around that long.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last two weeks where you felt okay, or has the sadness been constant?\n\nPatient: I’ve had little moments where I can act okay, I guess… like if I’m busy or I don’t want people to worry. But most days, honestly, I wake up with this heavy, flat sadness in my chest.\n\nIt’s like… I know I had plans for my life, and then it feels like there’s no point trying to build any of it. I’m sorry, that sounds dramatic. But it’s been there most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last two months where you felt significantly better, or has the sadness been constant?\n\nPatient: I mean… there have been small breaks, I guess, where I can distract myself and seem more normal. But I don’t think I’ve really felt significantly better.\n\nMost days I still wake up with that heavy, flat sadness in my chest… like there’s no point in trying to build the life I was planning. I’m sorry, I don’t mean to make it sound dramatic, but it’s been pretty constant.\n\nDoctor: Would you say this heavy, flat sadness is interfering with your daily life most of the time, or is it more of a mild feeling in the background?\n\nPatient: It’s… more than just in the background, I think. I don’t want to sound like I’m making excuses, but it’s been affecting things.\n\nAt work, I’m slower than I used to be, and I make these basic mistakes with salon routines that I really should know by now. My manager and some clients don’t really trust me the same way, and that feels awful. I’ve canceled things I used to handle without a problem.\n\nAnd outside of work I’ve kind of… pulled back from friends. I also stopped showing up for the volunteer haircuts at the senior center, which I used to be reliable with. So, yeah… I guess it is interfering most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: A little, maybe… I don’t want to say nothing, because that sounds bad. But it’s not like it used to be.\n\nI used to really like chatting with clients and helping the newer stylists at the salon, and I’d go hiking with my friends sometimes. Now I mostly just keep my head down and get through what I have to do. I kind of avoid people more than I mean to.\n\nIt’s not that I hate everything, I just… don’t really have that spark in me lately, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Would you say this sadness is mild, moderate, or severe most of the time?\n\nPatient: I’d probably say… moderate? I don’t know if that’s the right word.\n\nIt’s not like I’m completely falling apart every second, but most days I wake up with this heavy, flat sadness in my chest, like there’s no point in trying to build the life I thought I was working toward. So… yeah, I guess moderate most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but no longer find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess there are a few. I don’t want to make it sound like I’ve completely stopped caring, because I do still try, but it’s different now.\n\nI used to enjoy chatting with clients more, like actually connecting with them, and I liked helping the newer stylists learn little things. And hiking with my friends used to feel good too. Lately I mostly just keep my head down and get through the day, and I avoid people more than I probably should.\n\nIt’s like… I just don’t have that spark in me the way I used to, if that makes sense.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
